# M4.6c1 The Banks and the Command Dispatcher in C — Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Port the bank code and the command dispatcher to C and prove them against the ROM through `diff_lts`, `diff_serial` and the goldens. That covers `bank_load_ramcode`, the `@=` handler `bank_cmd_select`, the eight routines that run from the RAM copy, and `cmd_run_next`: 12 routines, 10 of them hooked.

**Architecture:**
- **Infrastructure first (Task 1).**
  - The firmware tracks the selected bank (`f->bank`) from its P2 writes. A ROM read under another bank goes to a new host call, `read_bank`.
  - Leaving through a bank entry is `fw_enter`, a new host call `enter` followed by an unwound exit.
  - The NUL-bug fallback uses `fw_rom_call_unhooked`.
  - Two machine API additions: `machine_hook_ramcode` (hooks in RAM code) and `machine_rom_call_unhooked`.
  - `diff_lts` compares a call that resets the stack at the bank entry. The suite `suite_fw_bank` is added.
- **The data (Task 2).** The RAM-code image `$ADFE–$AFFC` and the main bank's header become converted data: named tables plus an instruction listing that the data builder assembles.
- **The tests before the C (Task 3).** `diff_serial` gains probes for the cold entry, DIP bit 1 and two synthetic calls, proved ROM against ROM.
- **Then the C.** The RAM-code routines and `bank_load_ramcode` (Task 4), then `cmd_run_next` and `bank_cmd_select` (Task 5).
- **C calls C.** The C `cmd_run_due` calls the C `cmd_run_next`, which calls the ROM only for an unknown handler address.
- **What stays ROM.** The ROM `cmd_run_next`, for an unknown handler address only, through an unhooked ROM call; the bank-entry stubs `$FFD0`/`$FFD4`; boot, the main loop and `isr_idle` (M4.6c2); the host's wait loops.
- **Hooks.** 10 routines, giving 110 replacements and 111 `golden_hybrid` modes.

**Tech Stack:** C11 with MSVC 2022 (`build.bat`, `/W4 /WX`).

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-13-m4-6c1-banks-design.md` (M4.6c1 spec). Its foundations are:
- the M4.6b spec, `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-09-m4-6b-speech-timing-design.md` (the wait seam, P2 and the low window, `k_every_call`, the branch probe; §6 "As built");
- the M4.6a spec, `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-m4-6a-serial-design.md` (SFR access, `diff_serial` and its probes; §6 "As built");
- the M4.2 spec, `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-03-m4-2-command-parser-design.md` (the C dispatcher and its ROM fallback, the latent NUL bug, the unwound exit);
- the M4.0 spec, `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-03-m4-0-replacement-foundation-design.md` (§8 "As built").

The parent spec is `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-30-software-apollo2-design.md` (rev 16; §1.2 the memory map and P2 bank switching, §4 the machine model).

## Global Constraints

**Build and run**
- Build and test from the repo root with the Bash tool (Git Bash): `cmd //c ".\build.bat test [SUITE_FILTER]"`.
- In a git worktree, first run `export APOLLO2_ROM="C:/Users/abart/Desktop/Dolphin Apollo 2 ROM/UK_V2.01_4.04_ROM.BIN"`, because the ROM lives only in the main checkout.
- C11, `/W4 /WX`, `/wd4244` (8-bit truncation is intentional).
  - Fix warnings in code. Never add `/wd` flags or pragmas.
  - The only pragma allowed is the existing 4611 one in `thunks.c`. `setjmp` therefore stays out of the tests.
- Every `.c` file under `src/` and `tests/` must have a **unique basename**, because `build.bat` puts all objects in `build\`.
- Never run `build\apollo2.exe` without `-o FILE`, because it plays audio on the user's speakers.
- Audio stays 10 kHz native, with no resampling.
- The full suite takes about 12 minutes. Use a suite filter while iterating and the full run at each task's end, in the background (a foreground Bash call is capped at 600000 ms).

**What may and may not be committed**
- Never commit the ROM, any `*.BIN`/`*.bin`, `*.mp3`, `apollo2_excerpt.wav`, `*.sla`, `build/` or `apollo_ghidra/`.
- Committed files contain no ROM bytes as hex blobs. Converted data is readable C: numbers with their meaning, named bits, strings, named addresses. Code bytes are an instruction listing: one row per instruction with named operands, assembled by the data builder.
- Stage files by explicit path only. Never use `git add -A` or `git add .`.

**User-owned files and tools**
- Never stage, move or delete the user's untracked files: `docs/*` other than `docs/superpowers/`, `pics/`, `session_memory.json`, `apollo2_for_aksel.wav`, `apollo2_for_aksel.mp3`, `apollo2.png`.
- Never modify anything under `C:\ghidra_12.1.3_PUBLIC`, the user's Ghidra GUI project, or `ghidra740/` (including `ghidra740/coverage/`). Only read the annotations and the dumps.
- Disassembly dumps for checking the C against the ROM (git-ignored, read-only) are in the main checkout:
  - `C:\Users\abart\Desktop\Dolphin Apollo 2 ROM\build\ghidra740\dump\rom_<ADDR>_<name>.txt` and `ramcode_<ADDR>_<name>.txt`
  - `data.txt` and `functions.txt` in the same folder.

**Correctness rules**
- The goldens in `tests/golden/*.frm` stay byte-identical. No new golden. If anything changes a golden, stop and report.
- The machine model (parent spec §4) does not change. The only machine additions are API: `machine_hook_ramcode`, `machine_rom_call_unhooked`, and access to state for the comparisons.
- Nothing under `src/firmware/` includes a `machine/` or `cpu740/` header. `build.bat` checks this.
- The ported C keeps the ROM's behaviour exactly, quirks included (M4.6c1 spec §2.6):
  - every RAM write, the scratch ones included (`$75`/`$76`/`$78`, `$77`, `bank_absent` bit 0 set then cleared, the 14 ID bytes per bank, `bank_current`, `$01–$64` on the cold path, `$02FF` written twice);
  - the bank tables read from the RAM copy (`$0200,X`, `$0208,X`), never from the ROM image;
  - every SFR read and write in the ROM's order, through the host, each `SEB`/`CLB` as a read then a write;
  - every read of the selected bank through `fw_rom8`, so it reaches `read_bank` under another bank;
  - the 8-bit wraps and loop bounds exactly as the ROM has them;
  - the exit registers and flags the callers read, and at a bank entry the state `bank_enter` leaves (SP `$FF`, the I flag set, A and X).
- Writes to the dead stack (below SP at exit) need not be reproduced, because `diff_lts` and `diff_serial` exclude it.
- ROM data is read only through `fw_rom8` (or `fw_ind_y`/`fw_mem8`) at its ROM address. SFRs are accessed only through the `fw_sfr*` helpers.
- Thunk outputs: each with a comment saying what the ROM callers read after the call, checked in the dumps.
- For an unknown handler address the C leaves RAM untouched and calls the ROM `cmd_run_next` through `fw_rom_call_unhooked`. No other ROM call to firmware code is added.
- `diff_lts` and `diff_serial` failures are fixed by finding the root cause in the dump. Never loosen a comparison or an assertion.

**Commits**
- End every commit message with exactly `Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>`. This is the user's rule for this session.

## Review Focus

The inputs and conditions most likely to bite a user that the spec implies but no other test exercises. Each line has a test in the task that owns the code.

1. **`@=` with an argument outside 1–7** (`@=0`, `@=8`, `@=F`, `@=` followed by a letter that isn't a hex digit). The pattern `=#` takes a digit only, so `@=F` and `@=G` match no command and `cmd_hex_value` gives 0–9; `bank_select` indexes `$0200,X` past the 7 real entries: entry 0 is `$70`, the main ROM, and 8–9 read `bank_p2_table_alt[0–1]`, the main ROM too. The hybrid must enter, return or unwind exactly where the ROM does. Test: `diff_lts`'s `k_extra` texts and the CLI check (Task 5).
2. **Several `@=` in one input, and text after a warm re-entry** (`@=1 One. @=5 Two. @=1 Three.`). A warm re-entry abandons the C chain. No stale state may survive into the next clause: the trap, the bridge deadline, `f->bank`, `f->unwound`. Test: `diff_lts` `k_extra` and a `golden_hybrid` rom-against-hybrid comparison of that text (Task 5).
3. **`@L` after the banks change** (`@=5 @L`, and `@L` after a warm `@=1`). The reply reads `bank_id_table`, which the C bank code writes. The reply bytes the hybrid queues for transmission must equal the ROM's (the machine never raises the host line, so a reply stays in the output ring). Test: Task 5, comparing the queued reply bytes of both engines.
4. **Speech, then `@=`** (a long sentence that overwrites `$0200…` with segments, then `@=5`, then more speech). `bank_load_ramcode` must reload the tables before `bank_select` reads them. Test: `k_extra` (Task 5).
5. **A NUL byte after a command prefix in serial input** (`@A`, NUL, CR fed straight to the machine, never through the CLI). Neither engine may produce a frame. Where the target code does not return through the stack (`@M5` then NUL: `$002A`), the hybrid must end with the ROM engine's fault (a BRK at `$2300`). Where it does (`@A` then NUL: `$D300`), the C dispatcher's callers have no return addresses on the machine stack, so the hybrid faults at `$A893` while the ROM engine stops on an illegal opcode at `$8F02`. The hybrid on main already ends this way, and the user ruled to keep and pin it (2026-10-13). Test: Task 5, both engines on raw input.

### Task 1: Infrastructure: the selected bank (`f->bank`) and `read_bank`, `fw_enter`/`enter`, `fw_rom_call_unhooked`, `machine_hook_ramcode`, `machine_rom_call_unhooked`, the `ramcode` row flag, the bank and the window read back after a ROM call and a wait, `diff_lts` comparing a stack reset at the bank entry, `bank_addr.h`, the suite `suite_fw_bank` (spec §3, §4.1, §4.6, §4.8 item 1)

**ROM facts checked / what was verified while proving the plan** (`src/machine/machine.c`, `src/machine/m50734.c`, `src/cpu740/cpu740.c`; `ramcode_0210_bank_enter.txt`, `ramcode_025E_bank_select.txt`, `ramcode_0241_bank_scan.txt`, `rom_A61C_boot_reset.txt`, `rom_AC22_boot_init_ports.txt`, `rom_FFD0_bank_entry_cold.txt`, `rom_FFD4_bank_entry_warm.txt`, `rom_ADE0_boot_read_dip.txt`, `rom_ADEB_boot_init_settings.txt`, `rom_A7DF_cmd_clear_formant.txt`, `rom_A7F0_boot_load_prosody_defaults.txt`; the ROM image for the operand scans; everything below was run, nothing is copied from the spec):
- **The bank bits** (`machine_map_read`): $8000–$FFFF read the main ROM only while P2's pins bits 2–6 are $70; any other value is `MACHINE_MAP_EMPTY`, which `machine_peek` and the bus read as $FF (every socket but the main ROM is empty, spec §1). $4000–$7FFF are not banked (the window or the main file), so `fw_rom8` sends only $8000–$FFFF to `read_bank`. `boot_init_ports` writes P2 = $70 ($AC50 or $AC58, `LDM #$70,P2`) and `P2_DIR` = $FD: bits 2–6 are outputs, so the pins are the latch and a P2 write is the selection. The only P2 writes that change the bank bits are `bank_select`'s `STA P2` ($0273) and `bank_restore`'s ($0254); the window's `SEB7`/`CLB7` and `isr_dsp_reset`'s P2.0 keep them (read, then written back). Hence `f->bank` = bits 2–6 of every value written to P2, as `f->low_window` follows bit 7 (M4.6b). The test stubs of `test_fw_tn.c` and `test_fw_par.c` start P2 at 0, which `isr_dsp_reset`'s `SEB0`/`CLB0` now turn into bank 0 and a fault at the first image read above $8000: they start it at `P2_MAIN_BANK`, as the board does.
- **The bank entry** (`bank_enter` $021F–$023E): `LDX #$FF / TXS`; warm: `LDA $FFE1 / CMP $181B / BNE`, `LDA $FFE0 / CMP $1819 / BNE`, `JMP $FFD4` (A = the bank's $FFE0, X = $FF); cold: `LDA #0 / LDX #$64`, `STA $00,X / DEX / BNE` (zero page $64 down to $01), `JMP $FFD0` (A = 0, X = 0). The `SEI` at $0212 is not undone. From the `TXS` to the jump the warm path runs 6 instructions, the cold path at most 311, so `diff_run_to_bank_entry` steps at most `DIFF_ENTRY_STEPS` = 1000. At the entries the I flag masks interrupts until the `CLI` at $A66E; N, Z, C, V and Y are written before they are read on both re-entries (warm: $A66B `LDM`, `CLI`, `isr_par_hold` and `isr_par_ready` keep the registers, `cmd_apply_speaker`'s `par_setup_voice` writes Y; cold: `$FFD0 SEI / JMP $A665`, `boot_read_dip` loads A, `boot_init_settings`' `cmd_clear_formant` saves and restores Y, `boot_load_prosody_defaults` writes it): so the comparison at the entry takes A, X and the I flag (`diff_same_at_entry`), not Y or the other flags.
- **`machine_rom_call`'s unwound test** (`c->s > s0` after any instruction): `host_enter` sets SP $FF inside a thunk entered from a ROM call, so the enclosing call reports `MCALL_UNWOUND` at once and its C unwinds in turn (M4.2 spec 3), as for a ROM stack reset. `run_frame`'s unwound branch leaves the machine as it is: `host_enter` has set the PC to the entry.
- **`find_hook`**: a hook fires only where `machine_map_read` gives the main ROM file at the address itself; the RAM copy $0200–$03FE maps to RAM, so a `machine_hook` there never fires. `machine_hook_ramcode` marks its entry `ramcode`, and `find_hook` accepts such an entry where the map says RAM; the interrupt and wait tests before it are unchanged. `machine_rom_call_unhooked`'s skip is cleared by the first instruction that runs at the address (not by an interrupt taken there first: `find_hook` returns no hook then), and in any case when the call ends.
- **`diff_lts` with the bank-entry comparison and the ROM `cmd_run_next`** (Step 5): the 95 lines `<name> N calls checked` exactly as at 7435fa5 (diffed), `1 mid-clause flush calls skipped`, `2 unwound calls compared` (the `bank_select` `@=1` under `tn_convert_clause` and `cmd_run_due`, now compared at $FFD4 in both copies: the C copy's ROM call unwinds after the `TXS` at $0221 and is stepped on to the entry like the ROM copy). 175 s in the proof run.
- **Spec §3.2 gives `fw_enter(f, addr)`**: A and X must reach the host, so this plan passes them: `fw_enter(f, addr, a, x)` and `enter(ctx, addr, a, x)`. The hybrid host reads the bank back where it reads the window back: after a ROM call, after a wait, and at each thunk's entry (`sync_p2`).

No name collides: `grep -rn "read_bank\|fw_enter\|rom_call_unhooked\|P2_MAIN_BANK\|P2_BANK_BITS\|machine_hook_ramcode\|skip_hook\|fw_hook\|bank_addr\|suite_fw_bank\|diff_run_to_bank_entry\|diff_same_at_entry" src tests` prints only `machine.c`'s own private `#define P2_MAIN_BANK  0x70` and its use (line 24) before this task; `machine.c` includes no firmware header, so the two never meet.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

`tests/test_fw_bank.c` starts the suite with the helpers' tests (`f->bank`, `fw_rom8` under another bank, `fw_enter`, `fw_rom_call_unhooked` and their faults) on a stub host that logs every host call in order; Tasks 4–5 add the routines' tests to it. `test_machine_hooks.c` gains the machine calls' tests and the bridge's bank calls and read-back. `diff_state.c` gains the bank-entry stepping and comparison; `test_diff_lts.c` uses them for a call whose ROM copy reset the stack, hooks the rows through `fw_hook`, and grows `DIFF_MAX`. The stubs of `test_fw_tn.c` and `test_fw_par.c` start P2 at the main bank.

**Create `tests/test_fw_bank.c`:**

```c
/* Unit checks of the bank code and the command dispatcher (M4.6c1 spec 4.6): the firmware's selected bank
   (f->bank), ROM reads under another bank, the bank entry and the unhooked ROM call, with a stub host that logs, in
   order, every SFR read and write, every read of another bank, every bank entry and every unhooked ROM call. Each
   expected value is worked out from the dump of the routine (build/ghidra740/dump), as the comment beside it says. */
#include <string.h>
#include "test.h"
#include "firmware/bank_addr.h"
#include "firmware/data/fw_data.h"

#define MAX_LOG 512
#define SFR_P2  0xF0
#define SFR_P0  0xF6

typedef enum { EV_READ, EV_WRITE, EV_BANK, EV_ENTER, EV_CALL } ev_kind;
/* One host call: EV_READ/EV_WRITE an SFR (addr) and its value (a); EV_BANK a read of another bank at addr, the
   value (a) the stub returned; EV_ENTER the bank entry addr with a and x; EV_CALL an unhooked ROM call of addr with
   the registers a, x and y. */
typedef struct { ev_kind kind; uint16_t addr; uint8_t a, x, y; } event;

static uint8_t g_ram[0x10000];   /* the firmware's RAM is $0000-$3FFF; 64 KiB so no index can run off the array */
static uint8_t g_other[0x10000]; /* what read_bank returns: another bank's bytes ($FF: an empty socket) */
static fw g_f;
static fw_host g_host;
static uint8_t g_sfr[0x100];     /* the stub's SFRs: a read returns g_sfr[addr], a write sets it */
static event g_log[MAX_LOG];
static unsigned g_nlog;
static fw_call_result g_call_result;   /* what the stub's rom_call_unhooked returns */

static void log_add(ev_kind kind, uint16_t addr, uint8_t a, uint8_t x, uint8_t y)
{
    if (g_nlog < MAX_LOG)
        g_log[g_nlog] = (event){ kind, addr, a, x, y };
    g_nlog++;
}

static uint8_t stub_read_sfr(void *ctx, uint8_t addr)
{
    (void)ctx;
    log_add(EV_READ, addr, g_sfr[addr], 0, 0);
    return g_sfr[addr];
}

static void stub_write_sfr(void *ctx, uint8_t addr, uint8_t value)
{
    (void)ctx;
    g_sfr[addr] = value;
    log_add(EV_WRITE, addr, value, 0, 0);
}

static uint8_t stub_read_bank(void *ctx, uint16_t addr)
{
    (void)ctx;
    log_add(EV_BANK, addr, g_other[addr], 0, 0);
    return g_other[addr];
}

static void stub_enter(void *ctx, uint16_t addr, uint8_t a, uint8_t x)
{
    (void)ctx;
    log_add(EV_ENTER, addr, a, x, 0);
}

/* The unhooked ROM call: logged with its registers; it returns with A, X and Y each + 1 when g_call_result says so. */
static fw_call_result stub_rom_call_unhooked(void *ctx, uint16_t addr, fw_regs *r, uint64_t budget)
{
    (void)ctx;
    CHECK_EQ(budget, FW_ROM_CALL_BUDGET);
    log_add(EV_CALL, addr, r->a, r->x, r->y);
    r->a++;
    r->x++;
    r->y++;
    return g_call_result;
}

/* Zeroed RAM and SFRs, every other bank empty ($FF), the stub host (no hooked ROM call, no wait: the bank code makes
   neither), nothing logged. */
static void setup(void)
{
    memset(g_ram, 0, sizeof g_ram);
    memset(g_other, 0xFF, sizeof g_other);
    memset(g_sfr, 0, sizeof g_sfr);
    memset(&g_host, 0, sizeof g_host);
    g_host.ctx = &g_f;
    g_host.read_sfr = stub_read_sfr;
    g_host.write_sfr = stub_write_sfr;
    g_host.read_bank = stub_read_bank;
    g_host.enter = stub_enter;
    g_host.rom_call_unhooked = stub_rom_call_unhooked;
    g_nlog = 0;
    g_call_result = FW_CALL_RETURNED;
    fw_init(&g_f, g_ram, fw_image_get(), &g_host);
}

/* The log is exactly the n events of want. */
static void check_log(const event *want, unsigned n)
{
    CHECK_EQ(g_nlog, n);
    for (unsigned i = 0; i < n && i < g_nlog && i < MAX_LOG; i++) {
        CHECK_EQ(g_log[i].kind, want[i].kind);
        CHECK_EQ(g_log[i].addr, want[i].addr);
        CHECK_EQ(g_log[i].a, want[i].a);
        CHECK_EQ(g_log[i].x, want[i].x);
        CHECK_EQ(g_log[i].y, want[i].y);
    }
}

/* f->bank (M4.6c1 spec 3.1): P2_MAIN_BANK after fw_init; every P2 write through fw_wr_sfr8, fw_sfr_seb and fw_sfr_clb
   sets it to bits 2-6 of the value written (with the window from bit 7, M4.6b spec 3.4); other SFRs leave it. */
static void test_bank_tracking(void)
{
    static const event want[] = {
        { EV_WRITE, SFR_P2, 0xF9, 0, 0 },   /* the window, bank $78 (P2_BANK_5), P2.0 */
        { EV_READ, SFR_P2, 0xF9, 0, 0 },    /* CLB7 P2: a read, then a write */
        { EV_WRITE, SFR_P2, 0x79, 0, 0 },
        { EV_READ, SFR_P2, 0x50, 0, 0 },    /* SEB5 P2 with the pins at $50 (P2_BANK_2) */
        { EV_WRITE, SFR_P2, 0x70, 0, 0 },   /* the main bank */
        { EV_WRITE, SFR_P0, 0x10, 0, 0 },   /* not P2 */
    };
    setup();
    CHECK_EQ(g_f.bank, P2_MAIN_BANK);
    CHECK(!g_f.low_window);
    fw_wr_sfr8(&g_f, SFR_P2, 0xF9);
    CHECK_EQ(g_f.bank, P2_BANK_5);
    CHECK(g_f.low_window);
    fw_sfr_clb(&g_f, SFR_P2, 7);
    CHECK_EQ(g_f.bank, P2_BANK_5);
    CHECK(!g_f.low_window);
    g_sfr[SFR_P2] = 0x50;
    fw_sfr_seb(&g_f, SFR_P2, 5);
    CHECK_EQ(g_f.bank, P2_MAIN_BANK);
    fw_wr_sfr8(&g_f, SFR_P0, 0x10);
    CHECK_EQ(g_f.bank, P2_MAIN_BANK);
    check_log(want, 6);
    CHECK(!g_f.fault);
}

/* fw_rom8 (M4.6c1 spec 3.1): under the main bank $8000-$FFFF read the converted image; under another bank they go to
   the host's read_bank (an empty socket: $FF); $4000-$7FFF read the image under any bank. Without read_bank, or
   without a host, a read of $8000-$FFFF under another bank is a fault at the address. */
static void test_rom8_bank(void)
{
    static const event want[] = { { EV_BANK, 0xC561, 0x5A, 0, 0 }, { EV_BANK, 0xFFE0, 0xFF, 0, 0 } };
    setup();
    CHECK_EQ(fw_rom8(&g_f, 0xC561), '0');       /* ser_hex_chars[0], converted */
    CHECK_EQ(fw_rom8(&g_f, 0x4000), fw_image_get()->rom[0x4000]);
    CHECK_EQ(g_nlog, 0);
    g_f.bank = P2_BANK_5;
    g_other[0xC561] = 0x5A;
    CHECK_EQ(fw_rom8(&g_f, 0xC561), 0x5A);
    CHECK_EQ(fw_rom8(&g_f, 0xFFE0), 0xFF);      /* the empty socket */
    CHECK_EQ(fw_rom8(&g_f, 0x4000), fw_image_get()->rom[0x4000]);   /* not banked: no host call */
    check_log(want, 2);
    CHECK_EQ(fw_mem8(&g_f, 0x9800), 0xFF);      /* fw_mem8 reaches it too */
    CHECK_EQ(g_nlog, 3);
    CHECK(!g_f.fault);

    setup();
    g_host.read_bank = NULL;
    g_f.bank = P2_BANK_7;
    CHECK_EQ(fw_rom8(&g_f, 0xC561), 0xFF);
    CHECK(g_f.fault);
    CHECK_EQ(g_f.fault_addr, 0xC561);

    fw f;
    fw_init(&f, g_ram, fw_image_get(), NULL);
    f.bank = P2_BANK_7;
    CHECK_EQ(fw_rom8(&f, 0x9800), 0xFF);
    CHECK(f.fault);
    CHECK_EQ(f.fault_addr, 0x9800);
}

/* fw_enter (M4.6c1 spec 3.2): the host's enter with the address, A and X, then f->unwound (not a fault). Without
   enter, or without a host, a fault at the address and nothing entered. */
static void test_enter(void)
{
    static const event want[] = { { EV_ENTER, BANK_ENTRY_WARM, 0x02, 0xFF, 0 } };
    setup();
    fw_enter(&g_f, BANK_ENTRY_WARM, 0x02, 0xFF);
    check_log(want, 1);
    CHECK(g_f.unwound);
    CHECK(!g_f.fault);

    setup();
    g_host.enter = NULL;
    fw_enter(&g_f, BANK_ENTRY_COLD, 0, 0);
    CHECK(g_f.fault);
    CHECK_EQ(g_f.fault_addr, BANK_ENTRY_COLD);
    CHECK(!g_f.unwound);
    CHECK_EQ(g_nlog, 0);

    fw f;
    fw_init(&f, g_ram, fw_image_get(), NULL);
    fw_enter(&f, BANK_ENTRY_COLD, 0, 0);
    CHECK(f.fault);
    CHECK_EQ(f.fault_addr, BANK_ENTRY_COLD);
}

/* fw_rom_call_unhooked (M4.6c1 spec 3.2): the host's rom_call_unhooked with the registers, never its rom_call; a
   returned call gives the exit registers; a failed one is a fault at the address; an unwound one sets f->unwound.
   Without rom_call_unhooked, or without a host, a fault. */
static void test_rom_call_unhooked(void)
{
    static const event want[] = { { EV_CALL, 0xA893, 0x03, 0x03, 0x01 } };
    setup();
    fw_regs r = { 3, 3, 1, 0 };
    CHECK(fw_rom_call_unhooked(&g_f, 0xA893, &r, FW_ROM_CALL_BUDGET));
    check_log(want, 1);
    CHECK_EQ(r.a, 4);
    CHECK_EQ(r.x, 4);
    CHECK_EQ(r.y, 2);
    CHECK(!g_f.fault);

    setup();
    g_call_result = FW_CALL_FAILED;
    CHECK(!fw_rom_call_unhooked(&g_f, 0xA893, &r, FW_ROM_CALL_BUDGET));
    CHECK(g_f.fault);
    CHECK_EQ(g_f.fault_addr, 0xA893);

    setup();
    g_call_result = FW_CALL_UNWOUND;
    CHECK(!fw_rom_call_unhooked(&g_f, 0xA893, &r, FW_ROM_CALL_BUDGET));
    CHECK(g_f.unwound);
    CHECK(!g_f.fault);

    setup();
    g_host.rom_call_unhooked = NULL;
    CHECK(!fw_rom_call_unhooked(&g_f, 0xA893, &r, FW_ROM_CALL_BUDGET));
    CHECK(g_f.fault);
    CHECK_EQ(g_f.fault_addr, 0xA893);
    CHECK_EQ(g_nlog, 0);

    fw f;
    fw_init(&f, g_ram, fw_image_get(), NULL);
    CHECK(!fw_rom_call_unhooked(&f, 0xA893, &r, FW_ROM_CALL_BUDGET));
    CHECK(f.fault);
    CHECK_EQ(f.fault_addr, 0xA893);
}

void suite_fw_bank(void)
{
    CHECK(fw_image_get() != NULL);
    test_bank_tracking();
    test_rom8_bank();
    test_enter();
    test_rom_call_unhooked();
}
```

```c
/* The bridge's cycle deadline (thunks.h): a ROM call from C gets at most the cycles left before b->deadline, and
   none once it has passed; the call fails and fw_rom_call faults, as a call that does not return within its own
   budget does. 0 = no deadline. */
```

```c
    CHECK_EQ(g_m.unmodelled_count, 0);
}

static int g_count_a, g_count_b;

/* Counts in *user, then performs the routine: A = $77 and RTS. */
static bool hook_count_replace(machine *m, void *user)
{
    (*(int *)user)++;
    m->cpu.a = 0x77;
    machine_return(m);
    return true;
}

/* machine_hook_ramcode (M4.6c1 spec 3.3): a hook at a RAM-code address ($0200-$03FE) fires while the CPU executes
   the RAM there ($9000 JSR $0250, $0250: ASL A / ASL A / RTS in RAM); a main-ROM hook (machine_hook) at a RAM address
   never fires; addresses outside $0200-$03FE are refused, as is a second hook at a hooked address. */
static void test_hook_ramcode(void)
{
    static const uint8_t shl2[] = { 0x0A, 0x0A, 0x60 };
    load_main(0x0250);
    memcpy(g_m.ram + 0x0250, shl2, sizeof shl2);
    CHECK(machine_hook_ramcode(&g_m, 0x0250, hook_replace, NULL));
    run_to(&g_m, 0x9007);
    CHECK_EQ(g_m.ram[0x10], 0x77);
    CHECK_EQ(g_calls, 1);

    load_main(0x0250);
    memcpy(g_m.ram + 0x0250, shl2, sizeof shl2);
    CHECK(machine_hook(&g_m, 0x0250, hook_replace, NULL));
    run_to(&g_m, 0x9007);
    CHECK_EQ(g_m.ram[0x10], 0x14);               /* the RAM routine ran: the main-ROM hook did not fire */
    CHECK_EQ(g_calls, 0);

    load_main(0xC100);
    CHECK(!machine_hook_ramcode(&g_m, 0x01FF, hook_replace, NULL));
    CHECK(!machine_hook_ramcode(&g_m, 0x03FF, hook_replace, NULL));
    CHECK(!machine_hook_ramcode(&g_m, 0xC100, hook_replace, NULL));
    CHECK(machine_hook_ramcode(&g_m, MACHINE_RAMCODE_FIRST, hook_replace, NULL));
    CHECK(machine_hook_ramcode(&g_m, MACHINE_RAMCODE_LAST, hook_replace, NULL));
    CHECK(!machine_hook_ramcode(&g_m, MACHINE_RAMCODE_LAST, hook_replace, NULL));
    CHECK(!machine_hook(&g_m, MACHINE_RAMCODE_FIRST, hook_replace, NULL));
    CHECK_EQ(g_m.hook_count, 2);
}

/* machine_rom_call_unhooked (M4.6c1 spec 3.3): the hook at the called address is skipped at the routine's first
   instruction only. $C200: JSR $C100 / RTS; $C300: DEX / BEQ $C306 / JSR $C300 / RTS (it enters itself while X is
   not 0). The skipped hook does not fire, the hook at $C100 does; $C300's own hook fires on its second entry; the
   skip is gone after the call, and a plain machine_rom_call fires the hook. */
static void test_rom_call_unhooked(void)
{
    static const uint8_t call_c100[] = { 0x20, 0x00, 0xC1, 0x60 };
    static const uint8_t self[] = { 0xCA, 0xF0, 0x03, 0x20, 0x00, 0xC3, 0x60 };
    load_main(0xC100);
    put(0xC200, call_c100, sizeof call_c100);
    put(0xC300, self, sizeof self);
    CHECK(machine_init(&g_m, g_rom, sizeof g_rom, NULL));
    g_count_a = g_count_b = 0;
    CHECK(machine_hook(&g_m, 0xC100, hook_count_replace, &g_count_a));
    CHECK(machine_hook(&g_m, 0xC200, hook_count_replace, &g_count_b));
    machine_regs r = { 3, 0, 0, g_m.cpu.p };
    CHECK_EQ(machine_rom_call_unhooked(&g_m, 0xC100, &r, 1000), MCALL_RETURNED);
    CHECK_EQ(r.a, 12);                           /* ASL A / ASL A: the ROM routine ran */
    CHECK_EQ(g_count_a, 0);
    CHECK(!g_m.skip_hook);
    r.a = 3;
    CHECK_EQ(machine_rom_call_unhooked(&g_m, 0xC200, &r, 1000), MCALL_RETURNED);
    CHECK_EQ(g_count_b, 0);                      /* $C200's hook skipped ... */
    CHECK_EQ(g_count_a, 1);                      /* ... $C100's fired */
    CHECK_EQ(r.a, 0x77);
    r.a = 3;
    CHECK_EQ(machine_rom_call(&g_m, 0xC100, &r, 1000), MCALL_RETURNED);
    CHECK_EQ(g_count_a, 2);
    CHECK_EQ(r.a, 0x77);

    CHECK(machine_hook(&g_m, 0xC300, hook_count_replace, &g_count_b));
    machine_regs s = { 3, 2, 0, g_m.cpu.p };
    CHECK_EQ(machine_rom_call_unhooked(&g_m, 0xC300, &s, 1000), MCALL_RETURNED);
    CHECK_EQ(g_count_b, 1);                      /* the second entry of $C300 */
    CHECK_EQ(s.x, 1);
    CHECK_EQ(s.a, 0x77);
    CHECK_EQ(g_m.fault, MFAULT_NONE);
}

/* The bridge's bank calls (M4.6c1 spec 3.4): read_bank reads the bus as the CPU sees it (the main ROM while it is
   selected, $FF for an empty socket); fw_rom8 under another bank goes there; enter leaves the machine at the bank
   entry with SP $FF, I set, A and X; rom_call_unhooked runs the ROM routine past its own hook. */
static void test_host_bank(void)
{
    static fw_bridge b;
    load_main(0xC100);
    g_rom[0x9800] = 0x30;
    CHECK(machine_init(&g_m, g_rom, sizeof g_rom, NULL));
    CHECK(fw_bridge_init(&b, &g_m));
    CHECK_EQ(b.host.read_bank(b.host.ctx, 0x9800), 0x30);   /* P2 pulled to $70: the main ROM */
    fw_wr_sfr8(&b.f, M50734_P2_DIR, 0xFF);
    fw_wr_sfr8(&b.f, M50734_P2, P2_BANK_5);
    CHECK_EQ(b.f.bank, P2_BANK_5);
    CHECK_EQ(b.host.read_bank(b.host.ctx, 0x9800), 0xFF);   /* an empty socket */
    CHECK_EQ(fw_rom8(&b.f, 0xC561), 0xFF);                  /* converted, but another bank is selected */
    fw_wr_sfr8(&b.f, M50734_P2, P2_MAIN_BANK);
    CHECK_EQ(fw_rom8(&b.f, 0xC561), '0');                   /* the converted image again */
    CHECK(!b.f.fault);

    g_m.cpu.s = 0xE0;
    g_m.cpu.p = 0;
    fw_enter(&b.f, BANK_ENTRY_WARM, 0x02, 0xFF);
    CHECK(b.f.unwound);
    CHECK(!b.f.fault);
    CHECK_EQ(g_m.cpu.pc, BANK_ENTRY_WARM);
    CHECK_EQ(g_m.cpu.s, 0xFF);
    CHECK_EQ(g_m.cpu.p, CPU740_I);
    CHECK_EQ(g_m.cpu.a, 0x02);
    CHECK_EQ(g_m.cpu.x, 0xFF);

    load_main(0xC100);
    CHECK(fw_bridge_init(&b, &g_m));
    g_calls = 0;
    CHECK(machine_hook(&g_m, 0xC100, hook_replace, NULL));
    fw_regs r = { 3, 0, 0, 0 };
    CHECK(fw_rom_call_unhooked(&b.f, 0xC100, &r, FW_ROM_CALL_BUDGET));
    CHECK_EQ(r.a, 12);
    CHECK_EQ(g_calls, 0);
    r.a = 3;
    CHECK(fw_rom_call(&b.f, 0xC100, &r, FW_ROM_CALL_BUDGET));
    CHECK_EQ(r.a, 0x77);
    CHECK_EQ(g_calls, 1);
}

/* After a wait and after a ROM call the bridge reads the window and the bank back from the machine's P2 pins
   (M4.6b spec 3.4, M4.6c1 spec 3.1; the M4.6b deferred item: the read-back after a wait was untested). The timer
   interrupt's handler at $0300 (LDM #$F8,P2 / CLB3 ICR2 / RTI) selects bank $78 with the window on; a routine at
   $0310 (LDM #$70,P2 / RTS) selects the main bank with the window off. Both are in RAM and the wait is at $0320:
   an instruction in $8000-$FFFF under another bank would be a runaway PC. */
static void test_host_readback(void)
{
    static fw_bridge b;
    static const uint8_t isr_78[] = { 0x3C, 0xF8, 0xF0, 0x7F, 0xFE, 0x40 };
    static const uint8_t select_main[] = { 0x3C, 0x70, 0xF0, 0x60 };
    load_main(0xC100);
    vector(0xFFF8, 0x0300);
    CHECK(machine_init(&g_m, g_rom, sizeof g_rom, NULL));
    memcpy(g_m.ram + 0x0300, isr_78, sizeof isr_78);
    memcpy(g_m.ram + 0x0310, select_main, sizeof select_main);
    CHECK(fw_bridge_init(&b, &g_m));
    fw_wr_sfr8(&b.f, M50734_P2_DIR, 0xFF);
    fw_wr_sfr8(&b.f, M50734_P2, P2_MAIN_BANK);
    CHECK(!b.f.low_window);
    CHECK_EQ(b.f.bank, P2_MAIN_BANK);
    timer2_on(&g_m, true);
    g_m.cpu.p = 0;
    g_m.cpu.pc = 0x0320;
    fw_wait(&b.f, FW_WAIT_INTERRUPT, 0x0320);
    CHECK(!b.f.fault);
    CHECK(b.f.low_window);
    CHECK_EQ(b.f.bank, P2_BANK_5);

    fw_regs r = { 0, 0, 0, 0 };
    CHECK(fw_rom_call(&b.f, 0x0310, &r, FW_ROM_CALL_BUDGET));
    CHECK(!b.f.low_window);
    CHECK_EQ(b.f.bank, P2_MAIN_BANK);
}

/* The bridge's cycle deadline (thunks.h): a ROM call from C gets at most the cycles left before b->deadline, and
   none once it has passed; the call fails and fw_rom_call faults, as a call that does not return within its own
   budget does. 0 = no deadline. */
```

```c
/* A routine whose copy reset the stack (bank_enter's LDX #$FF / TXS) is compared where bank_enter jumps, at the bank
   entry BANK_ENTRY_WARM $FFD4 or BANK_ENTRY_COLD $FFD0 (M4.6c1 spec 4.1). diff_run_to_bank_entry steps m (at most
   DIFF_ENTRY_STEPS instructions) until its PC is one of them: at once when the C left it there (fw_enter); between
   the TXS and the jump the ROM only reads (warm) or clears $01-$64 (cold). False on a fault or when it gets there
   not. */
#define DIFF_ENTRY_STEPS 1000
bool diff_run_to_bank_entry(machine *m);
/* At a bank entry: diff_same with A and X, and the I flag (M4.6c1 spec 4.1). Y and the other flags are not compared:
   the warm and cold re-entries ($A66B, $A665) write each before reading it. */
bool diff_same_at_entry(const machine *a, const machine *b, char *why, size_t n);
```

```c
static bool at_bank_entry(const machine *m) { return m->cpu.pc == BANK_ENTRY_WARM || m->cpu.pc == BANK_ENTRY_COLD; }

bool diff_run_to_bank_entry(machine *m)
{
    for (int i = 0; i < DIFF_ENTRY_STEPS && !at_bank_entry(m); i++)
        if (!machine_step(m))
            return false;
    return at_bank_entry(m);
}

bool diff_same_at_entry(const machine *a, const machine *b, char *why, size_t n)
{
    if ((a->cpu.p ^ b->cpu.p) & CPU740_I) {
        snprintf(why, n, "I: ROM %d, C %d", (a->cpu.p & CPU740_I) != 0, (b->cpu.p & CPU740_I) != 0);
        return false;
    }
    return diff_same(a, b, FW_OUT_A | FW_OUT_X, why, n);
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

`bank_addr.h`, the host interface and its helpers (`fw.h`, `fw.c`), the two machine calls, the `ramcode` flag and `fw_hook`, and the hybrid host's three calls with the bank read back (`sync_p2`).

**Create `src/firmware/bank_addr.h`:**

```c
#ifndef FW_BANK_ADDR_H
#define FW_BANK_ADDR_H
/* Addresses of the bank code (M4.6c1 spec 2.1, 3.5), checked against build/ghidra740/dump and the JSR/JMP operands
   in the ROM image. bank_load_ramcode copies bank_ramcode_image $ADFE-$AFFC to RAM $0200-$03FE: the two bank tables
   and the routines below run from there (RAM address + BANK_RAMCODE_OFFSET = ROM address). A caller in parentheses
   stays ROM (M4.6c2). */

/* The RAM copy */
#define BANK_RAMCODE              0x0200   /* the copy's first byte, bank_p2_table */
#define BANK_P2_TABLE             0x0200   /* byte[8]: P2 per bank 0-7 while DIP bit 1 is clear (entry 0 unused) */
#define BANK_P2_TABLE_ALT         0x0208   /* byte[8]: the same while DIP bit 1 is set */
#define BANK_ENTER                0x0210   /* called by bank_cmd_select $ABB5 */
#define BANK_SCAN                 0x0241   /* called by (boot_reset $A67B) */
#define BANK_RESTORE              0x0252   /* bank_scan's tail, also bank_enter's exit ($021C JMP) */
#define BANK_SELECT               0x025E   /* called by bank_enter $0213, bank_scan $0246 */
#define BANK_PROBE                0x0277   /* called by bank_enter $0216, bank_scan $0249 */
#define BANK_READ_ID              0x0285   /* called by bank_scan $024C */
#define BANK_PUT_ID_CHAR          0x02E1   /* called by bank_read_id $029A, $02A5 ... $02CF (nine calls) */
#define BANK_SAFE_CHAR            0x02E9   /* called by bank_put_id_char $02E1 */
#define BANK_FIND_CURRENT         0x02F0   /* called by bank_cmd_select $ABB8 (and boot_reset $A67E) */
#define BANK_RAMCODE_LAST         0x03FE   /* the copy's last byte */

/* The ROM image of the copy */
#define BANK_RAMCODE_IMAGE        0xADFE   /* bank_ramcode_image: $ADFE-$AFFC */
#define BANK_RAMCODE_IMAGE_END    0xAFFC
#define BANK_RAMCODE_OFFSET       0xABFE   /* ROM address - RAM address of the copied bytes */

/* Main-ROM routines */
#define ROM_BANK_LOAD_RAMCODE     0xA600   /* called by bank_cmd_select $ABB2 (and boot_reset $A678) */
#define ROM_BOOT_REENTRY_COLD     0xA665   /* bank_entry_cold's JMP target (boot_reset's tail: boot_read_dip, ...) */
#define ROM_BOOT_REENTRY_WARM     0xA66B   /* bank_entry_warm's JMP target */

/* A bank's header (every bank has one at the same addresses; the main ROM's is converted data) */
#define BANK_ENTRY_COLD           0xFFD0   /* SEI / JMP: entered by bank_enter's JMP $FFD0 */
#define BANK_ENTRY_WARM           0xFFD4   /* JMP: entered by bank_enter's JMP $FFD4 */
#define BANK_SIGNATURE            0x78     /* bank_probe: a present bank's byte at BANK_ENTRY_COLD (the SEI opcode) */
#define BANK_HDR_VERSION          0xFFE0   /* byte[2]: the engine version major, minor */
#define BANK_HDR_LANG_CODE        0xFFE2   /* char[6]: ID part 1 */
#define BANK_HDR_LANG_VER_TEXT    0xFFEA   /* char[4]: ID part 3 */
#define BANK_HDR_ENGINE_VER_TEXT  0x9800   /* char[4]: ID part 2 */
#define BANK_ID_CHARS             14       /* bank_read_id: 6 + 4 + 4 characters per bank in bank_id_table */

/* P2 bank selections (bits 2-6), the values of bank_p2_table per bank number: P2_MAIN_BANK (fw.h) is banks 0 and 1 */
#define P2_BANK_2                 0x50
#define P2_BANK_3                 0x30
#define P2_BANK_4                 0x10
#define P2_BANK_5                 0x78
#define P2_BANK_6                 0x74
#define P2_BANK_7                 0x7C
#define P2_KEEP                   0x83     /* bank_select keeps P2 bits 0, 1 and 7 (AND #$83) */
#define DIP_ALT_BANKS             0x02     /* boot_dip bit 1: bank_select and bank_find_current read the alt table */

#endif
```

```c
bool machine_hook(machine *m, uint16_t addr, machine_hook_fn fn, void *user)
{
    if (m->hook_count == MACHINE_MAX_HOOKS || (m->hook_map[addr >> 3] & (1u << (addr & 7))))
        return false;
    m->hooks[m->hook_count].addr = addr;
    m->hooks[m->hook_count].fn = fn;
    m->hooks[m->hook_count].user = user;
    m->hook_count++;
    m->hook_map[addr >> 3] |= (uint8_t)(1u << (addr & 7));
    return true;
}

void machine_clear_hooks(machine *m)
{
    m->hook_count = 0;
    memset(m->hook_map, 0, sizeof m->hook_map);
}

void machine_return(machine *m)
```

```c
static bool add_hook(machine *m, uint16_t addr, machine_hook_fn fn, void *user, bool ramcode)
{
    if (m->hook_count == MACHINE_MAX_HOOKS || (m->hook_map[addr >> 3] & (1u << (addr & 7))))
        return false;
    m->hooks[m->hook_count].addr = addr;
    m->hooks[m->hook_count].fn = fn;
    m->hooks[m->hook_count].user = user;
    m->hooks[m->hook_count].ramcode = ramcode;
    m->hook_count++;
    m->hook_map[addr >> 3] |= (uint8_t)(1u << (addr & 7));
    return true;
}

bool machine_hook(machine *m, uint16_t addr, machine_hook_fn fn, void *user)
{
    return add_hook(m, addr, fn, user, false);
}

bool machine_hook_ramcode(machine *m, uint16_t addr, machine_hook_fn fn, void *user)
{
    if (addr < MACHINE_RAMCODE_FIRST || addr > MACHINE_RAMCODE_LAST)
        return false;
    return add_hook(m, addr, fn, user, true);
}

void machine_clear_hooks(machine *m)
{
    m->hook_count = 0;
    memset(m->hook_map, 0, sizeof m->hook_map);
    m->skip_hook = false;
}

void machine_return(machine *m)
```

```c
static bool window_on(const machine *m) { return (m50734_port_pins(&m->sfr, 2) & P2_LOWER_WINDOW) != 0; }

/* The firmware's view of P2 read back from the machine's pins (M4.6b spec 3.4, M4.6c1 spec 3.1): the window and the
   selected bank, after anything the ROM or an interrupt handler may have written. */
static void sync_p2(fw_bridge *b)
{
    b->f.low_window = window_on(b->m);
    b->f.bank = m50734_port_pins(&b->m->sfr, 2) & P2_BANK_BITS;
}
```

```c
static fw_call_result host_rom_call(void *ctx, uint16_t addr, fw_regs *r, uint64_t budget)
{

/* The NUL-bug fallback's ROM cmd_run_next (M4.6c1 spec 3.4): its own hook does not fire on this entry. */
static fw_call_result host_rom_call_unhooked(void *ctx, uint16_t addr, fw_regs *r, uint64_t budget)
{
    return rom_call(ctx, addr, r, budget, true);
}

/* A ROM byte under another bank (M4.6c1 spec 3.4): the bus as the CPU sees it, $FF for an empty socket. */
static uint8_t host_read_bank(void *ctx, uint16_t addr)
{
    return machine_peek(((fw_bridge *)ctx)->m, addr);
}

/* A bank entry (M4.6c1 spec 3.4): the machine goes on at addr with the state bank_enter leaves at its JMP: SP $FF
   (LDX #$FF / TXS), the I flag set (SEI), A and X. run_frame's unwound branch leaves the PC there. */
static void host_enter(void *ctx, uint16_t addr, uint8_t a, uint8_t x)
{
    machine *m = ((fw_bridge *)ctx)->m;
    m->cpu.s = 0xFF;
    m->cpu.p |= CPU740_I;
    m->cpu.a = a;
    m->cpu.x = x;
    m->cpu.pc = addr;
}
```

**`src/engine/thunks.c`**: replace

```c
typedef void (*thunk_regs)(fw *f, cpu740 *c);

/* The frame every thunk shares: set the fault trap (saving an outer thunk's, as a ROM call from C can reach
   another thunk on the same bridge), sync the window, run the routine; then restore the trap and either leave the
   machine where an unwound ROM call left it, report the fault, or emulate the RTS (an interrupt handler's RTI when
   isr is set, M4.6a spec 3.2). The outermost thunk (no deadline active) also bounds the whole call: b->deadline =
   its entry cycles + FW_ROM_CALL_SPEECH_BUDGET, cleared however it ends, so that a C wait that never ends
   (par_play's, a whole utterance inside one hook call) faults (thunks.h). */
```

```c
   either leave the machine where an unwound ROM call or a bank entry (fw_enter) left it, report the fault, or
   emulate the RTS (an interrupt handler's RTI when
   isr is set, M4.6a spec 3.2). The outermost thunk (no deadline active) also bounds the whole call: b->deadline =
   its entry cycles + FW_ROM_CALL_SPEECH_BUDGET, cleared however it ends, so that a C wait that never ends
```

```c
bool fw_install(machine *m, fw_bridge *b, const char *only)
{
    if (only) {
        const fw_replacement *r = fw_replacement_find(only);
        return r && machine_hook(m, r->addr, r->thunk, b);
    }
    for (size_t i = 0; i < fw_replacement_count; i++)
        if (!machine_hook(m, fw_replacements[i].addr, fw_replacements[i].thunk, b))
            return false;
    return true;
}
```

```c
bool fw_hook(machine *m, const fw_replacement *r, machine_hook_fn fn, void *user)
{
    return r->ramcode ? machine_hook_ramcode(m, r->addr, fn, user) : machine_hook(m, r->addr, fn, user);
}

bool fw_install(machine *m, fw_bridge *b, const char *only)
{
    if (only) {
        const fw_replacement *r = fw_replacement_find(only);
        return r && fw_hook(m, r, r->thunk, b);
    }
    for (size_t i = 0; i < fw_replacement_count; i++)
        if (!fw_hook(m, &fw_replacements[i], fw_replacements[i].thunk, b))
            return false;
    return true;
}
```

### Task 2: The data: the instruction listing and its encoder, `bank_data.c` with `bank_ramcode_image` $ADFE–$AFFC and the main bank's header, `firmware_data` green (spec §3.5, §4.8 item 2)

**ROM facts checked / what was verified while proving the plan** (`rom_A600_bank_load_ramcode.txt`, the eight `ramcode_*.txt`, `rom_AF11_lts_affix_next.txt`, `rom_AF1C_lts_affix_prev.txt`, `rom_FFD0_bank_entry_cold.txt`, `data.txt`, `ghidra740/annotations/lts.txt` and `bank.txt`; `src/cpu740/optable.c`; the ROM image, disassembled with a throwaway script built from that opcode table):
- **The copy** (`bank_load_ramcode` $A609–$A618): 256 passes of `LDA $ADFE,X / STA $0200,X` and `LDA $AEFD,X / STA $02FF,X`: $ADFE–$AFFC to $0200–$03FE, 511 bytes, $AEFD written twice (to $02FF). Its image: the two tables at $ADFE and $AE06 (`70 70 50 30 10 78 74 7C` and `70 70 78 74 7C 70 70 70`, written here as `P2_MAIN_BANK`, `P2_BANK_2` … `P2_BANK_7`: bank_p2_table_alt maps banks 2–4 to the selections of banks 5–7 and banks 5–7 to the main ROM), the bank routines $0210–$0312 at $AE0E–$AF10 (RAM address + $ABFE), 130 instructions, and the letter-to-sound code $AF11–$AFFC, 117 instructions from `lts_affix_next` to the `LDA #1` of `lts_sym_nonletter`, whose operand is at $AFFD, one byte past the copy. The listing ends with that instruction, so the converted bytes are $ADFE–$AFFD (512): the extra byte is ROM code and compares equal like the rest (spec §3.5 says $ADFE–$AFFC, the copy; the copy loop reads only those 511).
- **The header the bank routines read under the main bank**: $FFD0–$FFD3 `78 4C 65 A6` (`SEI` / `JMP boot_reentry_cold` $A665: the `SEI` is the signature `bank_probe` tests), `bank_hdr_version` $FFE0–$FFE1 = 2, 1, `bank_hdr_lang_code` $FFE2–$FFE7 = "000440", `bank_hdr_lang_ver_text` $FFEA–$FFED = "0404", `bank_hdr_engine_ver_text` $9800–$9803 = "0201" (the byte before `cmd_version_engine_text` $9804, converted in M4.1). `bank_hdr_ffe8` $FFE8–$FFE9 and `bank_entry_warm` $FFD4–$FFD7 are never read as data and stay unconverted. 20 bytes.
- **The opcodes**: the listings use 57 (mnemonic, mode) pairs of 42 mnemonics; each is the opcode `cpu740_optable` decodes to that mnemonic and mode (`test_listing_opcodes`), and a bit instruction's (`BBC`/`BBS` zp,rel and A,rel, `SEB`/`CLB` zp) is the bit-0 opcode + $20 × bit for each of the 8 bits. A branch's displacement is target − the address after the instruction, both in the listing's own addresses (RAM for the bank routines), which the constant offset keeps right in the ROM. `DEC A` is $1A (`FWM_ACC`).
- **The operands by name**: the zero-page and RAM operands are the `fw_ram.h` names (`BANK_ABSENT` $6E, `BOOT_DIP` $10, `BANK_CURRENT` $0E, `TN_RESUME_POS` $77, bank_select's scratch byte, `BANK_ID_TABLE` $2026, `BANK_VER_MAJOR`/`MINOR` $1819/$181B, `LTS_AFFIX_PTR` $1E, `TN_RULE_PTR` $20, `LTS_MATCH_POS` $4F, `BOOT_CLAUSE_LEN` $54, `LTS_MATCH_DIR` $6D, `CMD_TEXT` $1A00) and `fw.h`'s `FW_TMP_75`/`76`/`78`; the ROM operands `bank_addr.h`'s and `fw_data.h`'s (`LTS_SUFFIXES` $9825, `LTS_SIBILANTS` $9861, `LTS_AT_CONSONANTS` $9884, `LTS_CHAR_CLASS` $9AB4, `CC_VOICED`, `CC_CONSONANT`, `CC_FRONT_VOWEL`); the immediates `lts_sym_sibilant` and `lts_sym_at` load into Y are the index of their list's last character ($1F of 35 bytes, $14 of 24), `lts_sym_suffix`'s the index of its first entry (3, after `$00 CR LF`).
- **The proof run**: `firmware_data` prints `49044 converted bytes compared with the ROM, 0 differ` (48,512 + 512 + 20), the first time the listing was encoded.

```c
/* fw_opcodes against the emulator's 740 opcode table (src/cpu740/optable.c; M4.6c1 spec 3.5): each opcode decodes
   to its mnemonic and addressing mode, and a bit instruction's does for each of the 8 bits (opcode + $20 x bit). */
static void test_listing_opcodes(void)
{
    static const uint8_t k_mode[] = {
        [FWM_IMP] = AM_IMP, [FWM_ACC] = AM_ACC, [FWM_IMM] = AM_IMM, [FWM_ZP] = AM_ZP, [FWM_ZPX] = AM_ZPX,
        [FWM_ABS] = AM_ABS, [FWM_ABX] = AM_ABX, [FWM_ABY] = AM_ABY, [FWM_IZY] = AM_IZY, [FWM_REL] = AM_REL,
        [FWM_BIT_ZP] = AM_BIT_ZP, [FWM_BIT_A_REL] = AM_BIT_A_REL, [FWM_BIT_ZP_REL] = AM_BIT_ZP_REL,
    };
    const cpu740_opinfo *t = cpu740_optable();
    for (size_t k = 0; k < fw_opcode_count; k++) {
        const fw_opcode *o = &fw_opcodes[k];
        bool bit_op = o->mode == FWM_BIT_ZP || o->mode == FWM_BIT_A_REL || o->mode == FWM_BIT_ZP_REL;
        for (unsigned b = 0; b < (bit_op ? 8u : 1u); b++) {
            uint8_t op = (uint8_t)(o->opcode + 0x20 * b);
            CHECK_STR(cpu740_mnem_name(t[op].mnem), fw_mnem_names[o->mnem]);
            CHECK_EQ(t[op].mode, k_mode[o->mode]);
        }
        for (size_t j = 0; j < k; j++)
            CHECK(fw_opcodes[j].mnem != o->mnem || fw_opcodes[j].mode != o->mode);   /* each pair once */
    }
}

static void test_against_rom(const fw_image *img, const uint8_t *rom)
```

with

```c
   pros_pitch_ptr and pros_flags_ptr at, as references into fw_cmd_sound_effects and fw_cmd_sound_flags. */
typedef struct { const uint8_t *phoneme, *duration, *pitch, *flags; } fw_cmd_sound;

/* A code listing (M4.6c1 spec 3.5): one row per instruction at its address, its mnemonic (FWI_*) and addressing mode
   (FWM_*), the operand (an address, a zero-page address or an immediate) and, for a branch, the target address; a bit
   instruction (BBC, BBS, SEB, CLB) names its bit. The encoder (fw_data.c, step 12) assembles the rows with the
   opcodes of fw_opcodes. */
typedef enum {
    FWI_ADC, FWI_AND, FWI_BBC, FWI_BBS, FWI_BCC, FWI_BCS, FWI_BEQ, FWI_BNE, FWI_BRA, FWI_CLB, FWI_CLC, FWI_CMP,
    FWI_CPX, FWI_CPY, FWI_DEC, FWI_DEX, FWI_DEY, FWI_EOR, FWI_INC, FWI_INX, FWI_INY, FWI_JMP, FWI_JSR, FWI_LDA,
    FWI_LDX, FWI_LDY, FWI_NOP, FWI_ORA, FWI_PHA, FWI_PHP, FWI_PLA, FWI_PLP, FWI_RTS, FWI_SEB, FWI_SEI, FWI_STA,
    FWI_STX, FWI_STY, FWI_TAX, FWI_TAY, FWI_TXA, FWI_TXS, FWI_COUNT
} fw_mnem;
typedef enum {
    FWM_IMP,          /* no operand */
    FWM_ACC,          /* A (DEC A) */
    FWM_IMM,          /* #imm */
    FWM_ZP,           /* zp */
    FWM_ZPX,          /* zp,X */
    FWM_ABS,          /* abs */
    FWM_ABX,          /* abs,X */
    FWM_ABY,          /* abs,Y */
    FWM_IZY,          /* (zp),Y */
    FWM_REL,          /* a branch to target */
    FWM_BIT_ZP,       /* SEB/CLB bit,zp */
    FWM_BIT_A_REL,    /* BBC/BBS bit,A,target */
    FWM_BIT_ZP_REL,   /* BBC/BBS bit,zp,target */
} fw_mode;
typedef struct { uint16_t addr; uint8_t mnem, mode; uint16_t operand, target; uint8_t bit; } fw_insn;
/* The opcode of a mnemonic in an addressing mode; for a bit instruction, that of bit 0 (bit n adds $20 x n). */
typedef struct { uint8_t mnem, mode, opcode; } fw_opcode;

/* Pinned addresses (ghidra740/annotations: text_norm.txt, lts.txt) */
#define TN_CHAR_RULES_LO   0x4000   /* low byte of each character's list address */
#define TN_CHAR_RULES_HI   0x4100
```

```c
/* The opcodes of the code listings (M4.6c1 spec 3.5), from the 740 opcode table the emulator uses
   (src/cpu740/optable.c, after MAME's dm740.lst); test_firmware_data checks each against it. */
const fw_opcode fw_opcodes[] = {
    { FWI_ADC, FWM_IMM, 0x69 }, { FWI_AND, FWM_IMM, 0x29 }, { FWI_AND, FWM_ZP, 0x25 },
    { FWI_BBC, FWM_BIT_A_REL, 0x13 }, { FWI_BBC, FWM_BIT_ZP_REL, 0x17 }, { FWI_BBS, FWM_BIT_ZP_REL, 0x07 },
    { FWI_BCC, FWM_REL, 0x90 }, { FWI_BCS, FWM_REL, 0xB0 }, { FWI_BEQ, FWM_REL, 0xF0 }, { FWI_BNE, FWM_REL, 0xD0 },
    { FWI_BRA, FWM_REL, 0x80 }, { FWI_CLB, FWM_BIT_ZP, 0x1F }, { FWI_CLC, FWM_IMP, 0x18 }, { FWI_CMP, FWM_ABS, 0xCD },
    { FWI_CMP, FWM_ABX, 0xDD }, { FWI_CMP, FWM_IMM, 0xC9 }, { FWI_CPX, FWM_IMM, 0xE0 }, { FWI_CPX, FWM_ZP, 0xE4 },
    { FWI_CPY, FWM_IMM, 0xC0 }, { FWI_DEC, FWM_ACC, 0x1A }, { FWI_DEC, FWM_ZP, 0xC6 }, { FWI_DEX, FWM_IMP, 0xCA },
    { FWI_DEY, FWM_IMP, 0x88 }, { FWI_EOR, FWM_IMM, 0x49 }, { FWI_INC, FWM_ZP, 0xE6 }, { FWI_INX, FWM_IMP, 0xE8 },
    { FWI_INY, FWM_IMP, 0xC8 }, { FWI_JMP, FWM_ABS, 0x4C }, { FWI_JSR, FWM_ABS, 0x20 }, { FWI_LDA, FWM_ABS, 0xAD },
    { FWI_LDA, FWM_ABX, 0xBD }, { FWI_LDA, FWM_ABY, 0xB9 }, { FWI_LDA, FWM_IMM, 0xA9 }, { FWI_LDA, FWM_IZY, 0xB1 },
    { FWI_LDA, FWM_ZP, 0xA5 }, { FWI_LDX, FWM_IMM, 0xA2 }, { FWI_LDX, FWM_ZP, 0xA6 }, { FWI_LDY, FWM_IMM, 0xA0 },
    { FWI_LDY, FWM_ZP, 0xA4 }, { FWI_NOP, FWM_IMP, 0xEA }, { FWI_ORA, FWM_ZP, 0x05 }, { FWI_PHA, FWM_IMP, 0x48 },
    { FWI_PHP, FWM_IMP, 0x08 }, { FWI_PLA, FWM_IMP, 0x68 }, { FWI_PLP, FWM_IMP, 0x28 }, { FWI_RTS, FWM_IMP, 0x60 },
    { FWI_SEB, FWM_BIT_ZP, 0x0F }, { FWI_SEI, FWM_IMP, 0x78 }, { FWI_STA, FWM_ABX, 0x9D }, { FWI_STA, FWM_ZP, 0x85 },
    { FWI_STA, FWM_ZPX, 0x95 }, { FWI_STX, FWM_ZP, 0x86 }, { FWI_STY, FWM_ZP, 0x84 }, { FWI_TAX, FWM_IMP, 0xAA },
    { FWI_TAY, FWM_IMP, 0xA8 }, { FWI_TXA, FWM_IMP, 0x8A }, { FWI_TXS, FWM_IMP, 0x9A },
};
const size_t fw_opcode_count = sizeof fw_opcodes / sizeof fw_opcodes[0];

const char *const fw_mnem_names[FWI_COUNT] = {
    "ADC", "AND", "BBC", "BBS", "BCC", "BCS", "BEQ", "BNE", "BRA", "CLB", "CLC", "CMP", "CPX", "CPY", "DEC", "DEX",
    "DEY", "EOR", "INC", "INX", "INY", "JMP", "JSR", "LDA", "LDX", "LDY", "NOP", "ORA", "PHA", "PHP", "PLA", "PLP",
    "RTS", "SEB", "SEI", "STA", "STX", "STY", "TAX", "TAY", "TXA", "TXS",
};

/* The length of an instruction in mode: the opcode and its operand bytes. */
static unsigned insn_length(uint8_t mode)
{
    switch (mode) {
    case FWM_IMP: case FWM_ACC:
        return 1;
    case FWM_ABS: case FWM_ABX: case FWM_ABY: case FWM_BIT_ZP_REL:
        return 3;
    default:
        return 2;
    }
}

/* A branch's displacement from next (the address after the instruction) to target, which must fit a signed byte. */
static uint8_t branch(enc *e, const fw_insn *r, uint32_t next)
{
    int32_t d = (int32_t)r->target - (int32_t)next;
    if (d < -128 || d > 127)
        fail(e, "listing row $%04X: the branch to $%04X is out of reach", (unsigned)r->addr, (unsigned)r->target);
    return (uint8_t)d;
}

/* Assembles the n rows of a listing from at (each row's address + offset must be where the previous one ended) with
   the opcodes of fw_opcodes; returns the address after the last instruction. */
static uint32_t put_listing(enc *e, uint32_t at, const fw_insn *rows, size_t n, uint16_t offset)
{
    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; i++) {
        const fw_insn *r = &rows[i];
        if ((uint16_t)(r->addr + offset) != at)
            fail(e, "listing row $%04X lands at $%04X", (unsigned)r->addr, (unsigned)at);
        size_t k = 0;
        while (k < fw_opcode_count && (fw_opcodes[k].mnem != r->mnem || fw_opcodes[k].mode != r->mode))
            k++;
        if (k == fw_opcode_count) {
            fail(e, "listing row $%04X: no opcode for mnemonic %u in mode %u", (unsigned)r->addr, r->mnem, r->mode);
            return at;
        }
        bool bit_op = r->mode == FWM_BIT_ZP || r->mode == FWM_BIT_A_REL || r->mode == FWM_BIT_ZP_REL;
        if (r->bit > 7 || (r->bit && !bit_op))
            fail(e, "listing row $%04X: bit %u", (unsigned)r->addr, r->bit);
        unsigned len = insn_length(r->mode);
        uint32_t next = r->addr + len;   /* in the listing's own addresses, as the branch targets are */
        bool byte_operand = r->mode == FWM_IMM || r->mode == FWM_ZP || r->mode == FWM_ZPX || r->mode == FWM_IZY ||
                            r->mode == FWM_BIT_ZP || r->mode == FWM_BIT_ZP_REL;
        if (byte_operand && r->operand > 0xFF)
            fail(e, "listing row $%04X: operand $%04X is not a byte", (unsigned)r->addr, (unsigned)r->operand);
        put(e, false, at, (uint8_t)(fw_opcodes[k].opcode + 0x20 * r->bit));
        switch (r->mode) {
        case FWM_IMP: case FWM_ACC:
            break;
        case FWM_ABS: case FWM_ABX: case FWM_ABY:
            put_word(e, at + 1, r->operand);
            break;
        case FWM_REL: case FWM_BIT_A_REL:
            put(e, false, at + 1, branch(e, r, next));
            break;
        case FWM_BIT_ZP_REL:
            put(e, false, at + 1, (uint8_t)r->operand);
            put(e, false, at + 2, branch(e, r, next));
            break;
        default:
            put(e, false, at + 1, (uint8_t)r->operand);
            break;
        }
        at += len;
    }
    return at;
}

/* 12. The bank code (M4.6c1 spec 3.5): bank_ramcode_image from $ADFE, the two bank tables, the bank routines at their
   RAM addresses + BANK_RAMCODE_OFFSET, then the letter-to-sound code at its ROM addresses (to its last instruction's
   end, $AFFD); the main bank's header: bank_entry_cold assembled, bank_hdr_version, bank_hdr_lang_code and
   bank_hdr_lang_ver_text, and bank_hdr_engine_ver_text at $9800. The texts without C's terminating 0. */
static void put_bank_code(enc *e)
{
    uint32_t at = BANK_RAMCODE_IMAGE;
    for (int i = 0; i < 8; i++)
        put(e, false, at++, fw_bank_p2_table[i]);
    for (int i = 0; i < 8; i++)
        put(e, false, at++, fw_bank_p2_table_alt[i]);
    check_end(e, "bank_p2_table_alt", at, BANK_ENTER + BANK_RAMCODE_OFFSET);
    at = put_listing(e, at, fw_bank_ramcode, fw_bank_ramcode_count, BANK_RAMCODE_OFFSET);
    check_end(e, "the bank routines", at, BANK_RAMCODE_LTS + BANK_RAMCODE_OFFSET);
    at = put_listing(e, at, fw_bank_lts_code, fw_bank_lts_code_count, 0);
    check_end(e, "the letter-to-sound code", at, BANK_RAMCODE_IMAGE_END + 2);
    at = put_listing(e, BANK_ENTRY_COLD, fw_bank_entry_code, fw_bank_entry_code_count, 0);
    check_end(e, "bank_entry_cold", at, BANK_ENTRY_WARM);
    put(e, false, BANK_HDR_VERSION, fw_bank_hdr_version[0]);
    put(e, false, BANK_HDR_VERSION + 1, fw_bank_hdr_version[1]);
    check_end(e, "bank_hdr_lang_code", put_text(e, false, BANK_HDR_LANG_CODE, fw_bank_hdr_lang_code, false),
              BANK_HDR_LANG_CODE + BANK_LANG_CODE_CHARS);
    check_end(e, "bank_hdr_lang_ver_text", put_text(e, false, BANK_HDR_LANG_VER_TEXT, fw_bank_hdr_lang_ver_text, false),
              BANK_HDR_LANG_VER_TEXT + 4);
    check_end(e, "bank_hdr_engine_ver_text",
              put_text(e, false, BANK_HDR_ENGINE_VER_TEXT, fw_bank_hdr_engine_ver_text, false), CMD_VERSION_ENGINE_TEXT);
}

{
```

```c
/* Converted from the Apollo 2 ROM (M4.6c1 spec 3.5), then reviewed: bank_ramcode_image $ADFE-$AFFC, which
   bank_load_ramcode copies to RAM $0200-$03FE, and the main bank's header. Code is an instruction listing that the
   encoder (fw_data.c, step 12) assembles. tests/test_firmware_data.c re-encodes it and compares it byte for byte with
   the ROM. */
#include "fw_data.h"
#include "../bank_addr.h"

#define SFR_P2   0xF0   /* bank_select, bank_restore and bank_find_current read and write P2 */

/* The labels of the letter-to-sound code $AF11-$AFFD (ghidra740/annotations/lts.txt) */
#define LTS_AFFIX_NEXT          0xAF11
#define LTS_AFFIX_PREV          0xAF1C
#define LTS_AFFIX_MATCH_BACK    0xAF69
#define LTS_AFFIX_FIRST         3      /* lts_sym_suffix: the index of lts_suffixes' first entry (after $00 CR LF) */
#define LTS_SIBILANTS_LAST      0x1F   /* lts_sym_sibilant: the index of lts_sibilants' last character */
#define LTS_AT_CONSONANTS_LAST  0x14   /* lts_sym_at: the index of lts_at_consonants' last character */

/* bank_p2_table ($0200 in RAM) and bank_p2_table_alt ($0208): the P2 bank selection per bank number 0-7. bank_select
   reads them as $0200,X and $0208,X, so a bank number 8-15 reads the alt table through the first. */
const uint8_t fw_bank_p2_table[8] = {
    P2_MAIN_BANK,   /* 0: unused by bank_scan (1-7) */
    P2_MAIN_BANK, P2_BANK_2, P2_BANK_3, P2_BANK_4, P2_BANK_5, P2_BANK_6, P2_BANK_7,
};
const uint8_t fw_bank_p2_table_alt[8] = {
    P2_MAIN_BANK, P2_MAIN_BANK, P2_BANK_5, P2_BANK_6, P2_BANK_7, P2_MAIN_BANK, P2_MAIN_BANK, P2_MAIN_BANK,
};

/* The bank routines $0210-$0312, at their RAM addresses (ROM: + BANK_RAMCODE_OFFSET) */
const fw_insn fw_bank_ramcode[] = {
    /* bank_enter: A = bank 1-7; absent: bank_restore; else the stack reset and the warm or cold entry */
    { 0x0210, FWI_PHA, FWM_IMP },
    { 0x0211, FWI_PHP, FWM_IMP },
    { 0x0212, FWI_SEI, FWM_IMP },
    { 0x0213, FWI_JSR, FWM_ABS, BANK_SELECT },
    { 0x0216, FWI_JSR, FWM_ABS, BANK_PROBE },
    { 0x0219, FWI_BBC, FWM_BIT_ZP_REL, BANK_ABSENT, 0x021F, 0 },
    { 0x021C, FWI_JMP, FWM_ABS, BANK_RESTORE },
    { 0x021F, FWI_LDX, FWM_IMM, BANK_STACK_TOP },
    { 0x0221, FWI_TXS, FWM_IMP },
    { 0x0222, FWI_LDA, FWM_ABS, BANK_HDR_VERSION + 1 },
    { 0x0225, FWI_CMP, FWM_ABS, BANK_VER_MINOR },
    { 0x0228, FWI_BNE, FWM_REL, 0, 0x0235 },
    { 0x022A, FWI_LDA, FWM_ABS, BANK_HDR_VERSION },
    { 0x022D, FWI_CMP, FWM_ABS, BANK_VER_MAJOR },
    { 0x0230, FWI_BNE, FWM_REL, 0, 0x0235 },
    { 0x0232, FWI_JMP, FWM_ABS, BANK_ENTRY_WARM },
    { 0x0235, FWI_LDA, FWM_IMM, 0 },
    { 0x0237, FWI_LDX, FWM_IMM, BANK_ZP_CLEAR_LAST },
    { 0x0239, FWI_STA, FWM_ZPX, 0x00 },
    { 0x023B, FWI_DEX, FWM_IMP },
    { 0x023C, FWI_BNE, FWM_REL, 0, 0x0239 },
    { 0x023E, FWI_JMP, FWM_ABS, BANK_ENTRY_COLD },
    /* bank_scan: banks 7 down to 1, then its tail bank_restore $0252 */
    { 0x0241, FWI_PHA, FWM_IMP },
    { 0x0242, FWI_PHP, FWM_IMP },
    { 0x0243, FWI_SEI, FWM_IMP },
    { 0x0244, FWI_LDA, FWM_IMM, BANK_COUNT },
    { 0x0246, FWI_JSR, FWM_ABS, BANK_SELECT },
    { 0x0249, FWI_JSR, FWM_ABS, BANK_PROBE },
    { 0x024C, FWI_JSR, FWM_ABS, BANK_READ_ID },
    { 0x024F, FWI_DEC, FWM_ACC },
    { 0x0250, FWI_BNE, FWM_REL, 0, 0x0246 },

    { 0x0277, FWI_PHA, FWM_IMP },
```

```
  49044 converted bytes compared with the ROM, 0 differ
suite_firmware_data          ok
```

A byte that differs is a listing row with a wrong operand, mode or branch target: the message names the address; fix the row from the disassembly, never the comparison.

### Task 3: The tests before the C: `diff_serial`'s probes of the cold entry, DIP bit 1, a control code in a bank ID and P2 bank bits in neither table, proved ROM against ROM (spec §4.2, §4.8 item 3)

**ROM facts checked / what was verified while proving the plan** (`ramcode_0210_bank_enter.txt`, `ramcode_025E_bank_select.txt`, `ramcode_02E9_bank_safe_char.txt`, `ramcode_02F0_bank_find_current.txt`, `rom_ADE0_boot_read_dip.txt`, `rom_ADEB_boot_init_settings.txt`, `rom_C605_boot_selftest.txt`, `rom_AC22_boot_init_ports.txt`; `machine.c`; the throwaway branch probe of Task 5 Step 5):
- **The probe points**: the entries of three RAM-code routines, hooked on the corpus machine with `machine_hook_ramcode`: `bank_enter` $0210 (2 calls on `bank_select`: `@=5` and `@=1`; none elsewhere), `bank_find_current` $02F0 (at every boot, after `@=5` and after each warm re-entry), `bank_safe_char` $02E9 (14 per `bank_scan`: the main bank's ID; the six empty sockets store spaces without it). A probe's target destination in RAM is hooked on copy A with `machine_hook_ramcode` too.
- **The cold entry** (`$0222`–`$0230`): `LDA $FFE1 / CMP bank_ver_minor / BNE $0235`, then `LDA $FFE0 / CMP bank_ver_major / BNE $0235`; `boot_init_settings` copies $FFE0/$FFE1 to $1819/$181B ($ADF1–$ADFA), so on the corpus both are equal and every entry is warm (branch probe: $0228 and $0230 taken 0 times). The probes `cold_minor` (bank_ver_minor + 1) and `cold_major` (bank_ver_major + 1, the minor equal) call `bank_enter` with A = 1 at the `bank_enter` point: $0228 → $0235 and $0230 → $0235, then $01–$64 cleared and `JMP $FFD0`. Each copy's ROM call ends `MCALL_UNWOUND` at the `TXS`; both are stepped on to the entry and compared there (`diff_same_at_entry`, Task 1).
- **DIP bit 1** (`boot_read_dip` $ADE4: `boot_dip` = P4 & $0F, read once at boot): `bank_select` ($0263 `BBC1 boot_dip`) and `bank_find_current` ($02F9) read only `boot_dip`, never P4 (spec §2.6). With the P4 input's bit 1 set, `boot_selftest` ($C608 `BBC1 boot_dip`, $C60B `BBC1 P2`) passes while P2.1 is low; the default pins ($70) have it low, and `boot_init_ports` makes P2.1 an input (`P2_DIR` $FD). So `bank_select` is run once more with `port_in[4]` bit 1 set: at boot `bank_scan` reads `bank_p2_table_alt` (banks 7–5 and 1 the main ROM, 4–2 empty), `bank_find_current` matches at bank 1, and `@=5` reads `bank_p2_table_alt[5]` = $70: the main ROM, entered warm, as `@=1` is. The probes `dip_enter` (target $0263 → $0266, compared at $FFD4) and `dip_find` ($02F9 → $02FC) are checked in that run only; the other probes in the other runs only.
- **No bank matches** (`bank_find_current` $02F3–$030F): one read of P2 (`AND P2`), then X = 1 to 7 against the table `boot_dip` bit 1 picks at each pass; on the corpus the main bank matches at X = 1 every time ($0304 `BEQ` never falls through, $0309 `BCC` never runs). The probe `no_bank` sets P2's latch bits 2–6 to $0C, in neither table (the pins of outputs are the latch): $0309 → $030B, `bank_current` unchanged. The copy runs from RAM and returns to the sentinel in RAM, so the deselected main ROM is never executed.
- **A control code in an ID** (`bank_safe_char`: `CMP #$20 / BCS`): the main bank's ID "000440", "0201", "0404" has none ($02EB `BCS` taken 686 times, never not taken). The probe `safe_char` calls it with A = $1F: $02EB → $02ED.
- **None of the three routines is C yet**: `report()`'s check that every probe compares the C exempts exactly them (`k_not_c_yet`); Task 4 removes the list. The self-check runs `queries`, `bank_select` and `bank_select` with DIP bit 1 ROM against ROM; every probe compared and its target reached at every call, 0 mismatches (Step 2). The 28 probes of M4.6a and M4.6b now see `bank_select` in the self-check too (`queries` alone gave 23 and 9 calls).

**Files:**
- Modify: `tests/test_diff_serial.c`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 1's `machine_hook_ramcode`, `MACHINE_RAMCODE_FIRST`/`LAST`, `diff_run_to_bank_entry`, `diff_same_at_entry`, `bank_addr.h` (`BANK_ENTER`, `BANK_FIND_CURRENT`, `BANK_SAFE_CHAR`), `P2_BANK_BITS`; `machine_default_config`, `rom_engine_run` with a configuration.
- Produces: the probe points `AT_BANK_ENTER`, `AT_FIND_CURRENT`, `AT_SAFE_CHAR`; the probe fields `enters` and `dip`; the DIP bit 1 run (`run_dip`); the probes `cold_minor`, `cold_major`, `dip_enter`, `dip_find`, `no_bank`, `safe_char`; `k_not_c_yet` (Task 4 removes it).

```c
/* The probes whose routine is not C yet (M4.6c1 Task 3): copy B runs the ROM routine for them on the corpus too. */
static const char *const k_not_c_yet[] = { "bank_enter", "bank_find_current", "bank_safe_char" };

typedef enum { AT_INT1, AT_TX_PUT, AT_BANK_ENTER, AT_FIND_CURRENT, AT_SAFE_CHAR } probe_point;

typedef struct {
    const char *name;
    probe_point at;
    const char *routine;      /* the replacement copy B runs */
    uint16_t addr;            /* its entry (ROM, or RAM code) */
    bool isr;
    void (*inject)(machine *m);
    uint16_t branch, dest;    /* the target: the branch at branch goes to dest (branch 0: dest is reached) */
    bool enters;              /* bank_enter entering a bank: compared at the bank entry */
    bool dip;                 /* checked in the run with DIP bit 1 only (else in the other runs only) */
    unsigned calls, reached;
} probe;
```

```c
/* bank_enter with bank 1 (the main ROM, present) and a version byte of the running engine altered, so that the
   bank's header ($FFE1, then $FFE0) differs: the cold entry. bank_ver_minor; or bank_ver_major with the minor equal. */
static void inj_cold_minor(machine *m)
{
    m->cpu.a = 1;
    m->ram[BANK_VER_MINOR]++;
}
static void inj_cold_major(machine *m)
{
    m->cpu.a = 1;
    m->ram[BANK_VER_MAJOR]++;
}
/* bank_find_current with P2's bank bits in neither table: no match, bank_current unchanged */
static void inj_no_bank(machine *m)
{
    m->sfr.port_latch[2] = (uint8_t)((m->sfr.port_latch[2] & ~P2_BANK_BITS) | P2_NO_BANK);
}
/* bank_safe_char with a control code */
static void inj_safe_char(machine *m) { m->cpu.a = 0x1F; }

static probe g_probes[] = {
```

### Task 4: The bank code in C: `bank_load_ramcode` $A600 and the eight RAM-code routines `bank_enter` $0210, `bank_scan` $0241, `bank_select` $025E, `bank_probe` $0277, `bank_read_id` $0285, `bank_put_id_char` $02E1, `bank_safe_char` $02E9, `bank_find_current` $02F0, with their hooks; `bank_enter` and `bank_scan` call the C routines below them (spec §2.1–§2.4, §2.6, §4.1, §4.6, §4.8 item 4)

**ROM facts checked / what was verified while proving the plan** (`rom_A600_bank_load_ramcode.txt`, the eight `ramcode_*.txt`, `rom_ABAF_bank_cmd_select.txt`, `rom_A61C_boot_reset.txt`, `rom_A68D_boot_loop_text.txt`, `rom_A697_boot_get_clause.txt`, `rom_A880_cmd_run_due.txt`; the ROM image for the operand scans; the branch probe of Task 5 Step 5):
- **The callers** (the scan of every `JSR`/`JMP` operand in the ROM image and in the RAM copy; recorded in `bank_addr.h`, Task 1): `bank_load_ramcode` $A678 (boot_reset), $ABB2 (bank_cmd_select); `bank_enter` $ABB5; `bank_scan` $A67B; `bank_select` $0213, $0246; `bank_probe` $0216, $0249; `bank_read_id` $024C; `bank_put_id_char` $029A, $02A5, $02AB, $02B1, $02B7, $02BD, $02C3, $02C9, $02CF; `bank_safe_char` $02E1; `bank_find_current` $A67E, $ABB8; `bank_restore` $0252 only by `bank_enter`'s `JMP` $021C (it is bank_scan's tail). The byte before each entry is an `RTS`, data (`bank_p2_table_alt[7]` before $0210, $FF padding before $A600) or a `JMP` operand ($0240): no routine is entered by falling through.
- **The SFR accesses, in order**: `bank_load_ramcode` one read of P2 (`LDX P2` $A605); `bank_select` a read (`AND P2` $026C) then a write (`STA P2` $0273); `bank_restore` a write ($0254); `bank_find_current` one read ($02F7). No other SFR, and P4 is never read (the DIP bits come from `boot_dip`). The bank reads go through `fw_rom8`: `bank_probe` $FFD0; `bank_enter` $FFE1, then $FFE0 only when $FFE1 equals bank_ver_minor; `bank_read_id` $FFE2–$FFE7 (`LDA $FFE2,Y`), $9800–$9803, $FFEA–$FFED. The tables are read from the RAM copy (`LDA $0200,X`, `$0208,X`, `CMP $0200,X`, `$0208,X`).
- **The loops and their wraps**: `bank_read_id`'s `DEX / BEQ / CLC / ADC #14 / BRA` gives X = 14 × (A − 1) in 8 bits (A = 0 runs the add 255 times: 242); `STA $2026,X` / `INX` wraps X at 8 bits; `CPY #6 / BCC` and `CPY #14 / BCC` are unsigned; `bank_safe_char`'s `CMP #$20 / BCS` is unsigned ($80–$FF kept); `bank_find_current`'s `CPX #8 / BCC` stops after X = 7 and tests `boot_dip` bit 1 at each pass; `bank_scan`'s `DEC A / BNE` runs A = 7 down to 1; `bank_enter`'s cold loop `STA $00,X / DEX / BNE` clears $64 down to $01, not $00. `bank_select`'s index is A, so 8–15 read $0208–$020F (`bank_p2_table_alt`), and with `boot_dip` bit 1, $0210–$0217 (code bytes).
- **The exits** (what the callers read is in each row's comment, checked in the dumps): `bank_load_ramcode` A and Y kept, X = 0; `bank_select` A and Y kept, X = the table value; `bank_probe`, `bank_find_current` A, X and Y kept; `bank_read_id` A kept, X after the last byte written, Y = 6 (present) or 14 (absent); `bank_put_id_char` A through `bank_safe_char`, X + 1; `bank_scan` and an absent `bank_enter` A kept, X = $76, Y = $78 (`bank_restore`). No caller reads a flag: `PLP / PLA` leave N and Z from A, which `bank_find_current`'s and the boot code's next instructions overwrite. All nine rows declare A, X and Y. A present `bank_enter` leaves the C through `fw_enter` (Task 1).
- **The scratch writes**: $78 = Y, $76 = X, $75 = P2 (`bank_load_ramcode`, the bytes `bank_restore` reloads); $77 = P2 & $83 (`bank_select`; `TN_RESUME_POS`, whose other use is tn_convert_clause's); `bank_absent` bit 0 set, then cleared for a present bank (`SEB0`/`CLB0` on RAM: a read and a write, invisible but for the value); `$02FF` written twice with the same byte. The `fw.h` comments of `FW_TMP_75`/`76`/`78` name them.
- **Which hooks fire in the "all" mode**: from ROM callers only, so `bank_scan` and `bank_find_current` (boot_reset), and `bank_load_ramcode`, `bank_enter`, `bank_find_current` (the ROM `bank_cmd_select`, until Task 5). `bank_select`, `bank_probe`, `bank_read_id`, `bank_put_id_char` and `bank_safe_char` are reached from C only and fire in their own `golden_hybrid` mode (from the ROM `bank_scan` and `bank_enter`). In `bank_enter`'s mode `@=1` on `bank_select` unwinds (`runs_c_dispatch`).
- **`diff_lts`** with the nine rows in `k_every_call` (Step 4): `bank_enter 2`, `bank_scan 50`, `bank_select 352`, `bank_probe 352`, `bank_read_id 350`, `bank_put_id_char 700`, `bank_safe_char 700`, `bank_find_current 51`, `bank_load_ramcode 52` calls checked, `3 unwound calls compared` (`bank_enter`'s own `@=1` call joins the two of Task 1), 0 mismatches; 160 s in the proof run.

**Files:**
- Create: `src/firmware/bank.h`, `src/firmware/bank.c`
- Modify: `src/firmware/fw.h`, `src/engine/thunks.c`, `tests/test_fw_bank.c`, `tests/test_diff_lts.c`, `tests/test_diff_serial.c`, `tests/test_golden_hybrid.c`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 1's `fw_enter`, `f->bank`, `bank_addr.h`, the stub host of `test_fw_bank.c`; Task 2's image of $ADFE–$AFFD and the header; Task 3's probes; `ser_regs`-style register mapping (`thunks.c`).
